0: Not reached 
1: Reached 
 
By specifying key on, the bit which corresponds to that voice will become 0. 
Also, by writing an arbitrary value (any number other than zero) to this register, all bits are cleared to 0. 
 

===== PAGE 192 =====

 Low-Level Sound Library - Register Macros    8-59 
 PlayStation 2 IOP Library Reference Release 3.0.2  -  Sound Libraries 
SD_S_KOFF 
Key off (end sound generation) 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libsd 1.1 July 8, 2002 
Syntax 
u_int sceSdGetSwitch(SD_CORE_?|SD_S_KOFF); //Get 
void sceSdSetSwitch(SD_CORE_?|SD_S_KOFF, u_int value); //Set 
Description 
This register specifies the value of key off (end of sound generation) for each voice. Sound generation will 
be ended for each voice when the corresponding bit is set to 1. After the state changes to key off, the 
envelope will transition to release. Sound will not necessarily switch off immediately. 
Table 8-27 
Bit Symbol Contents 
0 VOICE Switch key off for voice 0 
…   
23 VOICE Switch key off for voice 23 
 
When this register is read, the value obtained is the previous value that was set. The value will not reflect 
voices that are actually performing key off processing. 
An interval of at least 2 Ts is required when continuously writing to the same register. When continuously 
writing with less than 2 Ts, the voice performing the actual end sound generation process is uncertain. 

===== PAGE 193 =====

8-60    Low-Level Sound Library - Register Macros 
PlayStation 2 IOP Library Reference Release 3.0.2  -  Sound Libraries 
SD_S_KON 
Key on (start sound generation) 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libsd 1.1 July 8, 2002 
Syntax 
u_int sceSdGetSwitch(SD_CORE_?|SD_S_KON); //Get 
void sceSdSetSwitch(SD_CORE_?|SD_S_KON, u_int value); //Set 
Description 
This register specifies the value of key on (i.e., start of sound generation) for each voice. Sound generation 
will be started for each voice when the corresponding bit is set to 1. Note that if a bit set to zero, it will not 
result in key off. 
Table 8-28 
Bit Symbol Contents 
0 VOICE Switch key on for voice 0 
…   
23 VOICE Switch key on for voice 23 
 
When this register is read, the value obtained is the previous value that was set. The value will not reflect 
voices that are actually producing sound. 
An interval of at least 2 Ts is required when continuously writing to the same register. When continuously 
writing with less than 2 Ts, the voice performing the actual end sound generation process is uncertain. 
Also, key on can be specified by writing bit 1 again without specifying key off to the voice performing the 
sound generation process.

SD_VA_LSAX 
Loop point address 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libsd 1.1 July 24, 2000 
Syntax 
u_int sceSdGetAddr(SD_CORE_?|SD_VOICE_?|SD_VA_LSAX); //Get 
void sceSdSetAddr(SD_CORE_?|SD_VOICE_?|SD_VA_LSAX,  
 u_int value); 
//Set 
Description 
This register indicates the top address of the block which is specified at the loop point in the waveform 
data. It is initially set after reaching the loop point block. 
During sound generation (after 4 Ts have passed following key on. Rewriting is ignored if less than 4 Ts) this 
register's value can be changed. However, in such cases, the address set to the corresponding voice takes 
precedence and the loop point block information is invalidated until the next key on for the voice. 
Table 8-32 
Bit Symbol Contents 
0-22 ADDR Address of loop point  
Bits 0-3 should be 0

===== PAGE 203 =====

8-70    Low-Level Sound Library - Register Macros 
PlayStation 2 IOP Library Reference Release 3.0.2  -  Sound Libraries 
SD_VP_PITCH 
Sound generation pitch 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libsd 1.1 July 8, 2002 
Syntax 
u_short sceSdGetParam(SD_CORE_?|SD_VOICE_?|SD_VP_PITCH); //Get 
void sceSdSetParam(SD_CORE_?|SD_VOICE_?|SD_VP_PITCH,  
 u_short value); 
//Set 
Description 
This register specifies the sound generation pitch for each voice. 
Table 8-38 
Bit Symbol Contents 
13-0 VALUE Specified pitch value 
 
If the pitch of the fundamental tone is f0, the relationship between the specified pitch value (VALUE) and the 
generated pitch f is as follows: 
f = VALUE * f0 / 4096 
In addition, if the sound source is used as a noise generator, there will be no change in auditory sensation 
when the specified pitch is varied. The pitch for the auditory sensation of noise is specified with the core 
setting wrapper. 
The pitch specification affects the performance of sound generation. When the specified pitch value is low, 
sound generation takes longer.

 Sound Data (HD) Simple Access Library - Structures    9-3
  
Structures 
SceSdHdProgramCommon 
Program basic information 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libsdhd 2.7 December 6, 2002 
Structure 
typedef struct{  
      unsigned int volume; Program volume (range: 0 - 128) 
      int panpot; Program panpot 
(range: 0 - 64 - 127 = left - center - right, minus 
indicates negative phase) 
      int transpose; Transpose (note value) 
      int detune; Detune (Note 1) 
} SceSdHdProgramCommon;  
(Note 1) detune is in units of 1/128 of a half-tone. 
Description 
This data structure is used for keeping basic program information. 
 
 PlayStation 2 IOP Library Reference Release 3.0.2  -  Sound Libraries

     unsigned int panCenter; Note number for which pan causes effect to be 0 
(range: 0 - 127) 
} SceSdHdProgramKeyFollow;

SceSdHdProgramLFO 
Program LFO information 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libsdhd 2.7 December 6, 2002 
Structure 
typedef struct{  
     unsigned int wavePitch; LFO waveform (pitch) (Note 2) 
     unsigned int waveAmp; LFO waveform (amp) (Note 2) 
     unsigned int startPhasePitch; LFO start phase (pitch) (Note 3) 
     unsigned int startPhaseAmp; LFO start phase (amp) (Note 3) 
     unsigned int phaseRandomPitch; LFO start phase random (pitch) 
     unsigned int phaseRandomAmp; LFO start phase random (amp) 
     unsigned int cyclePitch; LFO cycle (pitch) units: msec 
     unsigned int cycleAmp; LFO cycle (amp) units: msec 
     int pitchDepthUp; Pitch modulation amplitude+ (Note 1) 
     int pitchDepthDown; Pitch modulation amplitude- (Note 1) 
     int midiPitchDepthUp; MIDI pitch modulation maximum amplitude+ (Note 1) 
     int midiPitchDepthDown; MIDI pitch modulation maximum amplitude- (Note 1) 
     int ampDepthUp; Amp modulation amplitude+ 
     int ampDepthDown; Amp modulation amplitude- 
     int midiAmpDepthUp; MIDI modulation maximum amplitude+ 
     int midiAmpDepthDown; MIDI modulation maximum amplitude- 
} SceSdHdProgramLFO;  
 (Note 1) pitch modulation is in units of 1/128 of a half-tone 
 (Note 2) The LFO waveform is specified with the following constants. 
Table 9-1 
Constant LFO Waveform 
SCEHD_LFO_NON No LFO 
SCEHD_LFO_SAWUP Right rising sawtooth wave 
SCEHD_LFO_SAWDOWN Right falling sawtooth wave 
SCEHD_LFO_TRIANGLE Triangle wave 
SCEHD_LFO_SQUEARE Square wave 
SCEHD_LFO_NOISE Noise 
SCEHD_LFO_SIN Sine wave 
SCEHD_LFO_USER User-defined (not supported) 
 
(Note 3) The values that can be specified range from 0 to 255, representing 0 to 359 degrees. 
Description 
This structure holds program information related to the LFO.

SceSdHdSampleParam 
Sample information 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libsdhd 2.7 December 6, 2002 
Structure 
typedef struct{  
     int vagIndex; Link destination VAGInfo number (NO_CINDEX is 
noise) 
     unsigned int spuAttr; SPU-related Attribute (Note 1) 
     unsigned int lfoAttr; LFO-related Attribute (Note 2) 
     SceSdHdSampleVelRange velRange; Sample velocity range information 
     SceSdHdSampleVelFollow velFollow; Sample velocity Follow information 
     SceSdHdSampleCommon common; Sample basic information 
     SceSdHdSampleADSR ADSR; Sample ADSR information 
     SceSdHdSampleKeyFollow keyFollow; Sample KeyFollow information 
     SceSdHdSampleLFO LFO; Sample LFO information 
} SceSdHdSampleParam;  
 (Note 1) 
SCEHD_LFO_PITCH_KEYON When pitch LFO trigger is key on  
SCEHD_LFO_PITCH_KEYOFF When pitch LFO trigger is key off 
SCEHD_LFO_PITCH_BOTH When pitch LFO trigger is key on and key off 
SCEHD_LFO_AMP_KEYON When amp LFO trigger is key on

SCEHD_SPU_CORE_0 Select CORE0  
SCEHD_SPU_CORE_1 Select CORE1  
Description 
Sample information, which is linked from the sample set information, consists of data that indicates which 
waveform (VAGInfo information) is to be used as a function of the velocity range. It also includes information 
such as the envelope. When the VagIndex field value is NO_CINDEX (=-1), it means that noise generated 
by SPU2 is to be used instead of waveform data 
See also 
SceSdHdSampleVelRange, SceSdHdSampleVelFollow, SceSdHdSampleCommon,

9-12  Sound Data (HD) Simple Access Library - Structures     
SceSdHdSampleSetParam 
Sample set information 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libsdhd 2.7 December 6, 2002 
Structure 
typedef struct{  
     unsigned int velCurve; Velocity curve type 
     unsigned int velLimitLow; Lower limit of velocity for generating sound in this 
sample set 
     unsigned int velLimitHigh; Upper limit of velocity for generating sound in this 
sample set 
     unsigned int nSample; Number of samples belonging to this sample set 
} SceSdHdSampleSetParam;  
Description 
Sample set information, which is linked from the split block that contains program information, consists of 
data that indicates which sample's sound is to be generated as a function of the velocity range. It is used to 
represent generated waveforms that differ by key touch force (velocity) even for the same musical interval 
(split) of the same program. 
Use the following macros to specify the velocity curve type. For details, refer to the "Sound Data Formats" 
document. 
Table 9-2 
Macro Curve Type 
SCEHD_VEL_CURVE_B type0 
SCEHD_VEL_CURVE_B_REV type1 
SCEHD_VEL_CURVE_A type2 
SCEHD_VEL_CURVE_A_REV type3 
SCEHD_VEL_CURVE_C type4 
SCEHD_VEL_CURVE_C_REV type5 
See also 
sceSdHdGetSampleSetParam(), sceSdHdGetSampleParamAddr(), sceSdHdGetSampleSetParamByNote()

9-14  Sound Data (HD) Simple Access Library - Structures     
SceSdHdSampleVelRange 
Sample velocity range information 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libsdhd 2.7 December 6, 2002 
Structure 
typedef struct{  
     unsigned int low; Lower limit of generated sound velocity (range: 0 - 
127) 
     unsigned int crossFade; Starting velocity for crossfade (range: 0 - 127) 
     unsigned int high; Upper limit of generated sound velocity (range: 0 - 
127) 
} SceSdHdSampleVelRange;  
Description 
This structure holds sample information related to range (volume). 
When bit 7 of velRangeLow or velRangeHigh is set to 1, a crossfade will be specified for smoothing the 
boundary of the velocity range. Since the crossfade mechanism is similar to a split range (register), see the 
description of split range for more information. 
See also 
SceSdHdSplitRange, SceSdHdSampleParam 
 
PlayStation 2 IOP Library Reference Release 3.0.2  -  Sound Libraries

9-16  Sound Data (HD) Simple Access Library - Structures     
SceSdHdSplitBlock 
Split block 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libsdhd 2.7 December 6, 2002 
Structure 
typedef struct{  
     unsigned int sampleSetIndex; Link destination sample set number 
     unsigned int splitNumber; Split number 
     SceSdHdSplitRange range; Split range information 
     SceSdHdSplitBendRange bendRange; Split bend range information 
     SceSdHdSplitKeyFollow keyFollow; Split KeyFollow information 
     SceSdHdSplitCommon common; Split basic information 
} SceSdHdSplitBlock;  
Description 
A split block is a data structure for representing nuances of the generated sound or waveform that are due 
to changes in register in the program. A split block is included in program information. It contains data that 
represents the register (range of note numbers) and the sample set number that will be applied to that 
register, as well as other acoustic characteristics. 
See also 
SceSdHdSplitRange, SceSdHdSplitBendRange, SceSdHdSplitKeyFollow, SceSdHdSplitCommon, 
sceSdHdGetSplitBlock(), sceSdHdGetSplitBlockByNote() 
 
PlayStation 2 IOP Library Reference Release 3.0.2  -  Sound Libraries

SceSdHdSplitRange 
Split range information 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libsdhd 2.7 December 6, 2002 
Structure 
typedef struct{  
     unsigned int low; Lower limit note number for this split (range: 0 - 127) 
     unsigned int crossFade; Starting crossfade note number (range: 0 - 127) 
     unsigned int high; Upper limit note number for this split (range: 0 - 127) 
} SceSdHdSplitRange;  
Description 
This structure holds information related to the range (register) for a split block. 
When bit 7 of low or high is set to 1, a crossfade will be specified for smoothing the boundary of the 
register. For example, when bit 7 of high is set to 1, the sound generated by that split gradually decreases 
over high beginning at the crossFade, as shown in the following figure.

sceSdHdCheckProgramNumber 
Check program number usage 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libsdhd 2.7 December 6, 2002 
Syntax 
int sceSdHdCheckProgramNumber(  
 unsigned char *buffer, Starting address of HD data area 
 unsigned int programNumber) Program number 
Calling conditions 
Can be called from an interrupt handler  
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (does not depend on interrupt disabled or enabled state) 
Description 
This function returns SCE_OK when the program number is being used. 
Return value 
Table 9-4 
Value Result 
SCE_OK Normal termination 
< 0 Error 
 
When an error occurs, the low-order 16 bits of the return value indicate one of the following values 
depending on the type of error. 
Table 9-5 
Value Result 
SCE_EFAULT The starting address of the HD data area 
is invalid 
SCE_EFORMAT The HD data is invalid 
SCE_ENO_PROGRAM No program chunk was found 
SCE_EBAD_PNUM The program number is out of range 
SCE_ENO_PNUM The program number is not in use

   Sound Data (HD) Simple Access Library - Program and Split Block Functions  9-23
  
sceSdHdGetMaxProgramNumber 
Get maximum program number 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libsdhd 2.7 December 6, 2002 
Syntax 
int sceSdHdGetMaxProgramNumber(  
 unsigned char *buffer) Starting address of HD data area 
Calling conditions 
Can be called from an interrupt handler 
Call be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (does not depend on interrupt disabled or enabled state) 
Description 
Since program numbers can be set arbitrarily, if a program number is missing because it is not being used, 
the maximum value and the number of program numbers may not match. This function returns the 
maximum program number, not the number of program numbers that are being used. 
Return value 
The maximum program number is returned. 
When an error occurs, the low-order 16 bits of the return value indicate one of the following values 
depending on the type of error. 
Table 9-6 
Value Result 
SCE_EFAULT The starting address of the HD data area 
is invalid 
SCE_EFORMAT The HD data is invalid 
SCE_ENO_PROGRAM No program chunk was found 
See also 
sceSdHdGetValidProgramNumberCount(), sceSdHdGetValidProgramNumber()

Get all addresses of split blocks from note number 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libsdhd 2.7 December 6, 2002 
Syntax 
int sceSdHdGetSplitBlockAddrByNote(  
 unsigned char *buffer, Starting address of HD data area 
 unsigned int programNumber, Program number 
 unsigned int noteNumber, Note number 
 sceHardSynthSplitBlock **ptr) Pointer to destination for storing address that was 
obtained 
Calling conditions 
Can be called from an interrupt handler  
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (does not depend on interrupt disabled or enabled state) 
Description 
This function finds split block information corresponding to the note number for the program number and 
gets the relevant number of addresses of split block information. 
The address stored in ptr is the start of an array of sceHardSynthSplitBlock pointers and an area with a size 
large enough to hold the relevant number of pointers must be allocated in advance. 
The sceHardSynthSplitBlock structure is defined in hd.h. For details, refer to the "Sound Data Formats" 
document. 
If no corresponding split block information is found, or if this information could not be obtained, the function 
will return 0. 
Return value 
The number of split block addresses that were obtained is returned.

sample set information

SceSdHdSampleParam *param) Pointer to destination for storing acquired information

Can be called from a thread

Description 
This function finds sample information corresponding to the velocity for the sample set number, gets the 
relevant number of sets of sample information, and stores them in an array of SceSdHdSampleParam 
structures. 
param is the starting address of the SceSdHdSampleParam structure array, and an area with a size large

mode specifies whether or not to perform a check when searching the velocity range that has been set for

sceSdHdGetValidProgramNumberCount 
Get valid program number count 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libsdhd 2.7 December 6, 2002 
Syntax 
int sceSdHdGetValidProgramNumberCount(  
 unsigned char *buffer) Starting address of HD data area 
Calling conditions 
Can be called from an interrupt handler  
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (does not depend on interrupt disabled or enabled state) 
Description 
This function gets the number of sets of program information within the HD data. 
Since program numbers can be set arbitrarily, the maximum program number and the number of sets of 
program information may not match. This function gets the number of program numbers that are actually 
being used. 
Return value

sceSdHdModifyVelocity

Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libsdhd 2.7 December 6, 2002 
Syntax 
int sceSdHdVelocityConvert(  
 unsigned int curveType, Velocity curve type 
 int velocity) Velocity 
Calling conditions 
Can be called from an interrupt handler  
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (does not depend on interrupt disabled or enabled state) 
Description 
This function uses the velocity curve type indicated by velCurve to modify the velocity value. velCurve 
should contain the value of the velCurve member for the sample set information 
(SceSdHdSampleSetParam structure). For details about curve types, refer to the "Sound Data Formats" 
document. 
Return value

sceSdHdModifyVelocityLFO 
Get amount of LFO effect due to velocity curve 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libsdhd 2.7 December 6, 2002 
Syntax 
int sceSdHdVelocityConvertLFO(  
 unsigned int curveType, Velocity curve type 
 int velocity, Velocity 
 int center) Velocity for which amount of effect will be 0 
Calling conditions 
Can be called from an interrupt handler  
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (does not depend on interrupt disabled or enabled state) 
Description 
This function uses the velocity curve type and velocity value to get the amount of LFO effect. velCurve 
should contain the value of the velFollowPitchVelCurve or velFollowAmpVelCurve member for the sample 
information (SceSdHdSampleParam structure).  
The return value, which ranges from -65536 to 65536, represents an amount of LFO effect from -1 to 1. 
For details, see the "Sound Data Formats" document. 
Return value 
The amount of LFO effect corresponding to the velocity curve type and velocity is returned.  
The range of values is -1 (-65536) - 0 (0) - 1 (65535) 
See also 
SceSdHdSampleParam 
 PlayStation 2 IOP Library Reference Release 3.0.2  -  Sound Libraries

SceSdSqMidiData 
MIDI data information structure 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libsdsq 2.8 July 14, 2003 
Structure 
typedef struct{  
u_int readStatus; Read status (see details below) 
u_int midiNumber; MIDI data block number being used by this structure 
sceSeqMidiDataBlock *midiData; Address of data block (sceSeqMidiDataBlock) pointed to 
by midiNumber 
u_int offset; Offset to current data 
u_int nextOffset; Offset to next data 
u_int division; Resolution per quarter note 
u_int compMode; Compression mode (see details below) 
u_int compTableSize; Size of compressed table (0 for uncompressed mode) 
u_int deltaTime; Delta time converted to 32-bit fixed length 
u_char lastStatus; Running status 
u_char reserve[3]; Reserved area 
u_int messageLength; Restored data length 
u_char message[8]; Restored data (sequence (SMF) data) 
u_int originalMessageLength; SQ-format data length 
u_char originalMessage[12]; SQ-format data (delta time + sequence (SQ) data) 
} SceSdSqMidiData;  
 
readStatus is represented by the following constants. 
Table 10-1 
Macro Meaning 
SCESDSQ_READSTATUS_OK Structure is OK to use 
SCESDSQ_READSTATUS_END Read position reached the end 
 (structure cannot be used) 
SCESDSQ_READSTATUS_ERROR Read error (structure cannot be used) 
 
compMode (compression mode) is represented by the following constants. 
Table 10-2 
Macro Meaning 
SCESDSQ_COMPMODE_COMP Compressed 
 (Note On is overridden by polyphonic key 
pressure) 
SCESDSQ_COMPMODE_NOTCOMP Uncompressed 
 (Note On is not overridden by polyphonic 
key pressure) 
 
 PlayStation 2 IOP Library Reference Release 3.0.2 - Sound Libraries 

===== PAGE 283 =====

10-6   Sound Data (SQ) Simple Access Library - Structures    
Description 
This structure is used for getting MIDI data information from SQ data. 
Sequence data can be obtained one event at a time by first using the sceSdSqInitMidiData() function to 
initialize each member of the structure, and then calling sceSdSqReadMidiData(). 
This structure is also used as a read pointer when sceSdSqCopyMidiData() is used to save the current 
status. 
If the members of this structure are overwritten, there is no guarantee that the functions will work correctly. 
See also 
sceSdSqInitMidiData(), sceSdSqReadMidiData(), sceSdSqCopyMidiData() 
 
PlayStation 2 IOP Library Reference Release 3.0.2 - Sound Libraries 

===== PAGE 284 =====

Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libsdsq 2.8 July 14, 2003 
Structure 
typedef struct{

} SceSdSqSongData;  
 
readStatus is represented by the following constants. 
Table 10-3 
Macro Meaning 
SCESDSQ_READSTATUS_OK Structure is OK to use 
SCESDSQ_READSTATUS_END Read position reached the end 
 (structure cannot be used) 
SCESDSQ_READSTATUS_ERROR Read error (structure cannot be used) 
Description 
This structure is used to get information related to Song data from SQ data. 
Song commands can be obtained one event at a time by first using the sceSdSqInitSongData() function to 
initialize each member of the structure, and then calling sceSdSqReadSongData(). 
This structure is also used as a read pointer when sceSdSqCopySongData() is used to save the current 
status. 
If the members of this structure are overwritten, there is no guarantee that the functions will work correctly. 
See also 
sceSdSqInitSongData(), sceSdSqReadSongData(), sceSdSqCopySongData()

 Sound Data (SQ) Simple Access Library - Initialization Functions   10-9    
Initialization Functions 
sceSdSqInitMidiData 
Initialize MIDI data information structure 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libsdsq 2.8 July 14, 2003 
Syntax 
#include <sdsq.h>  
int sceSdSqInitMidiData(  
void *addr, SQ data address 
u_int midiNumber, MIDI data block number 
SceSdSqMidiData *midiData); Pointer to MIDI data information structure to be initialized 
Calling conditions 
Can be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread  
Multithread safe (does not depend on interrupt disabled or enabled state) 
Description 
This function initializes the MIDI data structure midiData for the SQ data specified by addr so that the MIDI 
data block specified by midiNumber can be read. 
The MIDI data information structure must be initialized before it can be used. You can check to see whether 
or not a MIDI data information structure has been properly initialized by checking its read status (the 
readStatus member of the MIDI data information structure). 
Return value 
Table 10-4 
Value Result 
SCE_OK Normal termination 
< 0 Error 
 
When an error occurs, the low-order 16 bits of the return value indicate one of the following values 
depending on the type of error. 
Table 10-5 
Value Result 
EFAULT The SQ data address is invalid 
EFORMAT The SQ data is invalid 
SCE_ENO_MIDI The MIDI chunk does not exist 
SCE_ENO_MIDINUM The specified MIDI data block number is 
not in use 
 PlayStation 2 IOP Library Reference Release 3.0.2 - Sound Libraries

10-12   Sound Data (SQ) Simple Access Library - Acquisition Functions   
Acquisition Functions 
sceSdSqGetCompTableDataByIndex 
Get compressed table data from index 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libsdsq 2.8 July 14, 2003 
Syntax 
#include <sdsq.h>  
int 
sceSdSqGetCompTableDataByIndex( 
 
void *addr, SQ data address 
u_int midiNumber, MIDI data block number for which data is to be obtained 
u_int compTableIndex, Index of compressed table for which data is to be 
obtained 
SceSdSqCompTableData *data Pointer to structure where compressed table data will be 
stored 
Calling Conditions 
Can be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread  
Multithread safe (does not depend on interrupt disabled or enabled state) 
Description 
This function first finds the compressed table in the MIDI data block specified by midiNumber, within the SQ 
data specified by addr. It then reads the entry specified by compTableIndex, and stores it in the 
compressed table structure data. 
The data size can be compressed by converting frequently occurring Note On messages in the SQ data to 
polyphonic key pressure message codes. A compressed table is a conversion table for performing this 
conversion process. For more information, please see the "Sound Data Formats" document. 
Return value 
Table 10-8 
Value Result 
SCE_OK Normal termination 
< 0 Error 
 
When an error occurs, the low-order 16 bits of the return value indicate one of the following values 
depending on the type of error. 
Table 10-9 
Value Result 
EFAULT The SQ data address is invalid 
EFORMAT The SQ data is invalid 
PlayStation 2 IOP Library Reference Release 3.0.2 - Sound Libraries 

===== PAGE 290 =====

 Sound Data (SQ) Simple Access Library - Acquisition Functions   10-13    
Value Result 
SCE_ENO_MIDI The MIDI chunk does not exist 
SCE_ENO_MIDINUM The specified MIDI data block number is 
not in use 
SCE_ENO_COMPTABLE The compressed table does not exist 
SCE_EBAD_COMPTABLEINDEX The specified index is invalid 
See Also 
SceSdSqCompTableData, sceSdSqGetMaxCompTableIndex()

sceSdSqGetCompTableOffset 
Get offset of compressed table 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libsdsq 2.8 July 14, 2003 
Syntax 
#include <sdsq.h>  
int sceSdSqGetCompTableOffset(  
void *addr, SQ data address 
u_int midiNumber, MIDI data block number for which offset is to be obtained 
u_int *offset); Location where offset will be stored 
Calling Conditions 
Can be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread  
Multithread safe (does not depend on interrupt disabled or enabled state) 
Description 
This function first finds the compressed table in the MIDI data block specified by midiNumber within the SQ 
data specified by addr. It then gets the start of the compressed table (the offset from the beginning of the 
SQ data), and stores it in the variable offset. 
The data size can be compressed by converting frequently occurring Note On messages in the SQ data to 
polyphonic key pressure message codes. A compressed table is a conversion table for performing this 
conversion process. For more information, please see the "Sound Data Formats" document. 
Return value 
Table 10-10 
Value Result 
SCE_OK Normal termination 
< 0 Error 
 
When an error occurs, the low-order 16 bits of the return value indicate one of the following values 
depending on the type of error. 
Table 10-11 
Value Result 
EFAULT The SQ data address is invalid 
EFORMAT The SQ data is invalid 
SCE_ENO_MIDI The MIDI chunk does not exist 
SCE_ENO_MIDINUM The specified MIDI data block number is 
not in use 
SCE_ENO_COMPTABLE The compressed table does not exist

sceSdSqGetMaxCompTableIndex 
Get maximum compressed table index 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libsdsq 2.8 July 14, 2003 
Syntax 
#include <sdsq.h>  
int sceSdSqGetMaxCompTableIndex(  
void *addr, SQ data address 
u_int midiNumber); Target MIDI data block number 
Calling Conditions 
Can be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread  
Multithread safe (does not depend on interrupt disabled or enabled state) 
Description 
This function finds the compressed table in the MIDI data block specified by midiNumber within the SQ data 
specified by addr and returns the maximum value of the index for that compressed table. 
The data size can be compressed by converting frequently occurring Note On messages in the SQ data to 
polyphonic key pressure message codes. A compressed table is a conversion table for performing this 
conversion process. For more information, please see the "Sound Data Formats" document. 
Return value 
The maximum value of the compressed table index is returned. 
When an error occurs, the value returned is negative and the low-order 16 bits indicate one of the following 
values depending on the type of error. 
Table 10-12 
Value Result 
EFAULT The SQ data address is invalid 
EFORMAT The SQ data is invalid 
SCE_ENO_MIDI The MIDI chunk does not exist 
SCE_ENO_MIDINUM The specified MIDI data block number is not in use 
SCE_ENO_COMPTABLE The compressed table does not exist 
See also 
sceSdSqGetCompTableDataByIndex()

10-16   Sound Data (SQ) Simple Access Library - Acquisition Functions   
sceSdSqGetMaxMidiNumber 
Get maximum MIDI data block number 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libsdsq 2.8 July 14, 2003 
Syntax 
#include <sdsq.h>  
int sceSdSqGetMaxMidiNumber(  
void *addr); SQ data address 
Calling conditions 
Can be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread  
Multithread safe (does not depend on interrupt disabled or enabled state) 
Description 
This function returns the largest block number among MIDI data blocks in the SQ data specified by addr. 
Since there may be cases in which MIDI data block numbers are unused (numbers may be missing), the 
largest MIDI data block number will not necessarily match the number of MIDI data blocks. 
Return value 
The maximum MIDI data block number is returned. 
When an error occurs, the value returned is negative and the low-order 16 bits indicate one of the following 
values depending on the type of error. 
Table 10-13 
Value Result 
EFAULT The SQ data address is invalid 
EFORMAT The SQ data is invalid 
SCE_ENO_MIDI The MIDI chunk does not exist 
 
 
PlayStation 2 IOP Library Reference Release 3.0.2 - Sound Libraries

sceSdSqReadMidiData 
Get one event’s worth of sequence data 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libsdsq 2.8 July 14, 2003 
Syntax 
#include <sdsq.h>  
int sceSdSqReadMidiData(  
SceSdSqMidiData *midiData); Pointer to MIDI data information structure 
Calling conditions 
Can be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (does not depend on interrupt disabled or enabled state) 
Description 
This function gets sequence data for the next event and stores it in the MIDI data structure midiData. 
The sequence data that this function obtains includes the raw sequence data in SQ format along with the 
delta time converted to a 32-bit fixed length and the sequence data restored to full SMF format. 
Return value 
Table 10-17 
Value Result 
SCE_OK Success 
< 0 Error 
 
When an error occurs, the low-order 16 bits of the return value indicate one of the following values 
depending on the type of error. 
Table 10-18 
Value Result 
SCE_EINIT The structure has not been initialized 
EFORMAT The SQ data is invalid 
See also 
SceSdSqMidiData, sceSdSqInitMidiData()

CSL Sound Effect SE Stream Generation - Management Functions    11-13  
sceSEIn_MakePitchOn                                             
Write Note On/Off message (specified pitch) to output port buffer 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
modsein 2.2 December 6, 2002 
Syntax 
int sceSEIn_MakePitchOn(  
sceCslCtx *module_context, Address of Module Context 
 unsigned int port_number, Output port number 
 unsigned int id, SE message ID 
 unsigned int bank_number, Bank number or sound effect timbre set number 
 unsigned int prog_number, Program number or sound effect timbre number 
 unsigned int note_number, Note number (0-127) 
 unsigned int velocity, Velocity (keypress intensity) (0-127: 0: note off) 
 int panpot, Panpot (-127 - 127: if negative, inverse specification) 
 unsigned int pitch) Generated pitch 
Calling conditions 
Can be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Not multithread safe (must be called in interrupt-disabled state) 
However, this function can be called in a multithreaded environment in an interrupt-enabled state if it does 
not conflict with other sceSEIn function calls or sceHSyn_Atick() calls. 
Description 
Writes Note On/Off message (specified pitch) to the specified output port buffer. 
Pitch is a value specified by SD_VP_PITCH (0 ~ 0x3fff) in the low-level sound library. 
In the current implementation, if sound generation is performed using this function, then the specification of 
PitchLFO in the bank binary data will be made ineffective. 
Notes 
In the current implementation, Time-Pitch cannot be performed for a voice if sound was generated by either 
a Note On/Off message (with pitch specification) or a Note on message (with pitch specification). To 
generate sound which will perform Time-Pitch processing, a Note On/Off message or Note on message 
must be used. 
Return value 
Table 11-6 
Value Macro Result 
0 sceSEInNoError Success 
-1 sceSEInError Error 
 
PlayStation 2 IOP Library Reference Release 3.0.2  -  Sound Libraries

11-14    CSL Sound Effect SE Stream Generation - Management Functions     
sceSEIn_MakePitchOnZero                                       
Write Note On message (with pitch specification) to output port buffer 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
modsein 2.4.2 December 6, 2002 
Syntax 
int sceSEIn_MakePitchOnZero(  
 sceCslCtx *module_context, Address of Module Context 
 unsigned int port_number, Output port number 
 unsigned int id, SE message ID 
 unsigned int bank_number, Bank number or sound effect timbre set number 
 unsigned int prog_number, Program number or sound effect timbre number 
 unsigned int note_number, Note number (0-127) 
 unsigned int velocity, Velocity (key strike intensity) (0-127) 
 int panpot, Panpot (-127 - 127: if negative, inverse specification) 
 unsigned int pitch) Generated pitch 
Calling conditions 
Can be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Not multithread safe (must be called in an interrupt-disabled state) 
However, this function can be called in a multithreaded environment in an interrupt-enabled state if it does 
not conflict with other sceSEIn function calls or sceHSyn_Atick() calls. 
Description 
This function writes a Note On message (with pitch specification) to the specified output port buffer.  
When velocity is 0, the voice is still assigned and sound is generated, but with volume 0. To mute this 
sound, use sceSEIn_MakePitchOn(). 
pitch is the value that is specified by SD_VP_PITCH in the low-level sound library (range is 0-0x3fff). 
In the current implementation, when sound is generated by this function, the PitchLFO specification in the 
bank binary data is ignored. 
Notes 
In the current implementation, Time-Pitch cannot be performed for a voice if sound was generated by either 
a Note On/Off message (with pitch specification) or a Note On message (with pitch specification). To 
generate sound that will perform Time-Pitch processing, a Note On/Off message or Note On message 
must be used. 
Return value

Value Macro Result 
0 sceSEInNoError Success 
-1 sceSEInError Error

int sceSEIn_PitchOn(  
 sceCslCtx *module_context, Address of Module Context 
 unsigned int port_number, Output port number 
 unsigned int id, SE message ID 
 unsigned int bank_number, Bank number or sound effect timbre set number 
 unsigned int prog_number, Program number or sound effect timbre number 
 unsigned int note_number, Note number

 int panpot, Panpot (-127 - 127: if negative, inverse specification)

CSL Sound Effect SE Stream Generation - Management Functions    11-23  
sceSEIn_GetErxEntries 
Get library entries (EE only) 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
modsein 2.8 July 14, 2003 
Syntax 
void *sceSEIn_GetErxEntries (void);  
Calling Conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (does not depend on interrupt disabled or enabled state) 
Description 
This function gets a pointer to the SceErxLibraryHeader structure which is needed to register the libsein 
library in liberx. 
If the SceErxLibraryHeader structure returned by this function is passed to the 
sceErxRegisterLibraryEntries() function of liberx, the statically linked libsein library can be called from an 
ERX module (can be dynamically linked).  
When this function is linked, the entire implementation of libsein will be linked via the library entry table of 
liberx. 
In some cases, this may also increase the size of the program. 
Return value 
The entry library for libsein is returned. 
See also 
sceErxInit() 
 
PlayStation 2 IOP Library Reference Release 3.0.2  -  Sound Libraries

Structure

 unsigned char port; Output port number 
} sceSESqPortAssignment;  
Description

12-6  CSL Sound Effect SE Sequencer - Interrupt Processing Function       
Interrupt Processing Function 
sceSESq_ATick 
Periodic data conversion processing 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
modsesq 2.1 March 29, 2002 
Syntax 
int sceSESq_ATick(  
 sceCslCtx *module_context) Address of Module Context 
Calling conditions 
Can be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Not multithread safe (must be called in an interrupt-disabled state) 
However, this function can be called in a multithreaded environment in an interrupt-enabled state if it does 
not conflict with other sceSESq function calls or sceHSyn_ATick() calls. 
Description 
This function is periodically called after a certain time interval. 
Each time this function is called, the SE sequence to be performed proceeds by converting a portion of it 
over the time interval specified by the interval argument in the sceSESq_Init() function, to an SE stream. 
Return value 
Always returns sceSESqNoError (0). 
See also 
SceSESq_Init() 
 
PlayStation 2 IOP Library Reference Release 3.0.2  -  Sound Libraries

CSL Sound Effect SE Sequencer - Management Functions    12-11 
sceSESq_SeqIsDataEnd 
Get SE sequence state (is end ofdata?) 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
modsesq 2.1 March 29, 2002 
Syntax 
Bool sceSESq_SeqIsDataEnd(  
 sceCslCtx *module_context, Address of Module Context 
 unsigned int port_number, Input port number 
 int sesq_id) Sequence ID 
Calling conditions 
Can be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (does not depend on interrupt disabled or enabled state) 
Description 
This function checks whether or not the SE sequence of the specified sequence ID has reached the end of 
data. 
This function can also be used for an SE sequence that is not being performed, even if the performance 
has been stopped midway. This is because the determination is made based on the performance position 
at the time of the interruption.  
While sound is being generated for SE sequence data, if the end of data is reached without the sound 
being muted, there may be sound generation remaining even if this function returns True. To stop sound 
generation for the affected voice, use sceSESq_SeqPlaySwitch() or sceSESq_SeqTerminateVoice() with the 
setting of sceSESq_SeqPlayTerminate that meets the allocation conditions for the sequence ID. 
Return value 
Table 12-5 
Value Macro Result 
1 True Reached the end of the data 
0 False Did not reach the end of the data 
-1 sceSESqError Error 
 
 PlayStation 2 IOP Library Reference Release 3.0.2  -  Sound Libraries 

===== PAGE 337 =====

12-12  CSL Sound Effect SE Sequencer - Management Functions       
sceSESq_SeqIsInPlay 
Get SE sequence state (is performing?) 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
modsesq 2.1 March 29, 2002 
Syntax 
Bool sceSESq_SeqIsInPlay(  
 sceCslCtx *module_context, Address of Module Context 
 unsigned int port_number, Input port number 
 int sesq_id) Sequence ID 
Calling conditions 
Can be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (does not depend on interrupt disabled or enabled state) 
Description 
This function checks whether or not the SE sequence of the specified sequence ID is being performed. 
When SE sequence processing reaches the end of data, this function returns False to indicate the end of 
performance for that SE sequence.  However, while sound is being generated for SE sequence data, if the 
end of data is reached without the sound being muted, there may be sound generation remaining even if 
this function returns False. To stop sound generation for the affected voice, use sceSESq_SeqPlaySwitch() 
or sceSESq_SeqTerminateVoice() with the setting of sceSESq_SeqPlayTerminate that meets the allocation 
conditions for the sequence ID. 
Return value 
Table 12-6 
Value Macro Result 
1 True Reached the end of the data 
0 False Did not reach the end of the data 
-1 sceSESqError Error 
 
PlayStation 2 IOP Library Reference Release 3.0.2  -  Sound Libraries

CSL Sound Effect SE Sequencer - Management Functions    12-13 
sceSESq_SeqPlaySwitch 
Start or stop performance of SE sequence 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
modsesq 2.1 March 29, 2002 
Syntax 
int sceSESq_SeqPlaySwitch(  
 sceCslCtx *module_context, Address of Module Context 
 unsigned int port_number, Input port number 
 int sesq_id, Sequence ID 
 int command) Performance command 
sceSESq_SeqPlayStop               Stop performance 
sceSESq_SeqPlayStart               Start performance 
sceSESq_SeqPlayTerminate Explicitly terminate the  
                                                    performance 
sceSESq_SeqPlayStart              Control operation by Oring in the  
                                                    bit shown below 
sceSESq_SeqPlaySeqIDAutoUnselect    Automatically cancel the  
                                                               sequence ID assignment  
                                                               after the performance ends
Calling conditions 
Can be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Not multithread safe (must be called in an interrupt-disabled state) 
However, this function can be called in a multithreaded environment in an interrupt-enabled state if it does 
not conflict with other sceSESq function calls. 
Description 
This function starts/stops a performance. 
When the performance start is specified with sceSESq_SeqPlayStart and at the same time, 
sceSESq_SeqPlaySeqIDAutoUnselect is ORed into command, unselect processing for the specified 
sequence ID will be automatically performed internally to cancel the assignment when the performance of 
the SE sequence reaches the end of data or when the performance is explicitly terminated with 
sceSESq_SeqPlayStop/Terminate.  
If this function is called with command set to sceSESq_SeqPlayStop, and if the performance of the SE 
sequence had already reached the end of the data and stopped, then no processing is performed. 
To mute any voices which are still producing sound after the performance has ended, call this function with 
command set to sceSESq_SeqPlayTerminate.  
Also, to mute any voices which are still producing sound after the sequence ID assignment was canceled 
with sceSESq_UnselectSeq() or when sceSESq_SeqPlaySeqIDAutoUnselect was specified when the 
performance was started, set the SE message ID for the SE sequence with sceSESq_SeqSetSEMsgID() 
before the performance is started and call sceSESq_SeqTerminateVoice(). 
 PlayStation 2 IOP Library Reference Release 3.0.2  -  Sound Libraries 

===== PAGE 339 =====

Return value 
If processing is successful, returns sceSESqNoError (0). 
If there is an error, returns sceSESqError (-1). Review the environment or arguments. 
  
PlayStation 2 IOP Library Reference Release 3.0.2  -  Sound Libraries

CSL Sound Effect SE Sequencer - Management Functions    12-15 
sceSESq_SeqSetSEMsgID                                           
Specify SE message ID that SE sequence will use 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
modsesq 2.3 March 29, 2002 
Syntax 
int sceSESq_SeqSetSEMsgID(  
sceCslCtx *module_context, Module Context address 
unsigned int port_number, Input port number 
int sesq_id, Sequence ID 
unsigned int se_message_id) SE message ID 
Calling conditions 
Can be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Not multithread safe (must be called in an interrupt-disabled state) 
However, this function can be called in a multithreaded environment in an interrupt-enabled state if it does 
not conflict with other sceSESq function calls. 
Description 
This function sets the SE message ID (4 bytes) that the SE sequence specified by the sequence ID sesq_id 
will use. 
Note: this function can be used any time after the sequence ID has been assigned with 
sceSESq_SelectSeq() until the performance is started with sceSESq_SeqPlaySwitch().  
If the sequence ID assignment is canceled with sceSESq_UnselectSeq(), the SE message ID that was 
specified by this function will also be disabled for the relevant sequence ID. 
Return value 
If processing is successful, returns sceSESqNoError (0). 
If there is an error, returns sceSESqError (-1). Review the environment or arguments. 
         
 PlayStation 2 IOP Library Reference Release 3.0.2  -  Sound Libraries

12-16  CSL Sound Effect SE Sequencer - Management Functions       
sceSESq_SeqTerminateVoice                                      
Explicitly terminate voices for which sound was produced by SE sequence 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
modsesq 2.3 March 29, 2002 
Syntax 
int sceSESq_SeqTerminateVoice (  
sceCslCtx *module_context, Module Context address 
unsigned int in_port_number, Input port number 
unsigned int out_port_number, Output port number 
unsigned int se_message_id, SE message ID 
unsigned int mask) Mask 
Calling conditions 
Can be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Not multithread safe (must be called in interrupt-disabled state) 
However, this function can be called in a multithreaded environment in an interrupt-enabled state if it does 
not conflict with other sceSESq function calls. 
Description 
This function explicitly terminates voices for which sound was produced by an SE sequence. 
The specified se_message_id is ANDed with the specified mask and compared with the AND of the 
specified mask and the SE message IDs kept by the voices.  All voices where the result is the same will be 
explicitly terminated. 
Note: this function should only be used to mute voices which are still producing sound after the assignment 
of the sequence ID (sesq_id) is canceled with sceSESq_UnselectSeq(). 
When a sequence ID is reused, sceSESqSeqSetSEMsgID() should be used in advance to set the SE 
message ID that a module will use for that sequence ID. 
Once a sequence ID assignment is valid, set the performance command to sceSESq_SeqPlayStop or 
sceSESq_SeqPlayTerminate when calling sceSESq_SeqPlaySwitch(). 
Return value 
If processing is successful, returns sceSESqNoError (0). 
If there is an error, returns sceSESqError (-1). Review the environment or arguments. 
 
PlayStation 2 IOP Library Reference Release 3.0.2  -  Sound Libraries 

===== PAGE 342 =====

sceSESq2ATick 
Periodic data conversion processing 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
modsesq2 3.0.1 March 31, 2004 
Syntax 
int sceSESq2ATick(  
 sceCslCtx *module_context) Address of Module Context 
Calling conditions 
Can be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Not multithread safe (must be called in interrupt-disabled state)  
Note that this function can be called in a multithreaded environment in an interrupt-enabled state if it does 
not conflict with other sceSESq2 function calls or with calls to sceHSyn_ATick(). 
Description 
This function is called at regular time intervals. 
Whenever this function is called, part of the SE sequence to be performed is converted to an SE stream 
over the time interval specified by the interval argument of the sceSESq2_Init() function, allowing the SE 
sequence to advance. 
Return value 
Table 13-1 
Value Result 
SCE_OK Normal termination 
< 0 Error

Table 13-2 
Value Result 
SCE_EREGISTER Address of the ID monitor structure has not been registered

sceSESq2Load 
Load sequence data 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
modsesq2 3.0.1 March 31, 2004 
Syntax 
int sceSESq2Load(  
 sceCslCtx *module_context, Address of Module Context 
 u_int port_number) Input port number 
Calling conditions 
Can be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (does not depend on interrupt disabled or enabled state) 
Description 
This function registers SQ sequence data in the specified input port. 
If a performance is in progress for the specified input port, proper operation cannot be guaranteed if that 
input port's environment attributes are changed, or if sceSESq2_Load() is called. 
The members of sceSESq2Env that are initialized by this function and their values are shown below.

Member Value 
songNum sceSESq2Env_NoSeSongNum (currently, not valid) 
masterVolume sceSESq2_Volume0db 
masterPanpot sceSESq2_PanpotCenter 
masterTimeScale sceSESq2_BaseTimeScale 
status 0 
defaultOutPort sceSESq2Env_NoOutPortAssignment 
outPort [].setNo sceSESq2Env_NoSeqSet; 
outPort [].port sceSESq2Env_NoOutPortAssignment 
Return value 
Table 13-8 
Value Result 
SCE_OK Normal termination 
< 0 Error

13-16       CSL Sound Effect Sequencer 2 - Control Functions 
 
sceSESq2Play 
Start performance of sound effect sequence 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
modsesq2 3.0.1 July 26, 2004 
Syntax 
int sceSESq2Play(  
 sceCslCtx *module_context, Address of Module Context 
 u_int port_number, Input port number 
 u_char setNo, Sound effect sequence set number 
 u_char seqNo, Sound effect sequence number 
 u_char volume, Starting volume (0~127)  
 u_char panpot, Starting panpot (0~127)  
 short pitch) Starting pitch (-1200~1200)  
Calling conditions 
Can be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Not multithread safe (must be called in interrupt-disabled state)  
Note that this function can be called in a multithreaded environment in an interrupt-enabled state if it does 
not conflict with other sceSESq2 function calls. 
Description 
The message ID in the return value of this function is used to control the volume, panpot, and pitch. 
Pitch is given in units of cents. There are 100 cents in a halftone. 
The message ID, which is automatically allocated, is deleted either when the performance ends (one shot) 
or when the performance is stopped. 
0xF8AABBBB   
F8 sceSESq2MessageIdPrefix:  Currently, 0xf8 
AA modsesq2 input port number which is specified by the sq data 
and to which the target SE sequence belongs 
BBBB Automatically allocated in the range 0~65535 
 
A maximum of 32 sequences can be performed simultaneously by one port. 
Return value 
Table 13-14 
Value Result 
0xf8000000-0xf8ffffff Message ID 
Other than above Error 
 
If an error occurs, the low-order 16 bits of the return value indicate one of the following values. 
PlayStation 2 IOP Library Reference Release 3.0.2  -  Sound Libraries

Library Introduced Documentation last modified

Calling conditions 
Can be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Not multithread safe (must be called in interrupt-disabled state)  
Note that this function can be called in a multithreaded environment in an interrupt-enabled state if it does 
not conflict with other sceSESq2 function calls. 
Description 
This function is similar to the sceSESq2Play() function, except that it does not use a starting volume, 
starting panpot, or starting pitch when it starts a performance. 
In this case, when the performance starts, these parameters take on the values set in the HD and SQ data.

13-20       CSL Sound Effect Sequencer 2 - Control Functions 
 
sceSESq2Pause 
Pause sound effect sequence being performed 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
modsesq2 3.0.1 March 31, 2004 
Syntax 
int sceSESq2Pause(  
 sceCslCtx *module_context, Address of Module Context 
 u_int message_id, Message ID 
 u_int mode) sceSESq2_Stop: Stop sequences only 
sceSESq2_AllStop: Also stop looped sound 
Calling conditions 
Can be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Not multithread safe (must be called in interrupt-disabled state)  
Note that this function can be called in a multithreaded environment in an interrupt-enabled state if it does 
not conflict with other sceSESq2 function calls. 
Description 
This function pauses the performance of the sound effect sequence specified by the message ID. 
When mode is set to sceSESq2_Stop, only sequences are stopped.  
When mode is set to sceSESq2_AllStop, looped sounds are also stopped and are not restarted when the 
performance is restarted. 
Return value 
Table 13-20 
Value Result 
SCE_OK Normal termination 
 
If an error occurs, the low-order 16 bits of the return value indicate one of the following values. 
Table 13-21 
Value Result 
EINVAL Invalid argument 
EFAULT Invalid Module Context address 
EFORMAT Invalid Module Context  
SCE_EREGISTER Address of the ID monitor structure has not been registered 
SCE_ENOT_PLAYED Specified message_id is not being performed

13-22       CSL Sound Effect Sequencer 2 - Control Functions 
 
sceSESq2SetVolume 
Set volume of sound effect sequence 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
modsesq2 3.0.1 March 31, 2004 
Syntax 
int sceSESq2SetVolume(  
 sceCslCtx *module_context, Address of Module Context 
 u_int message_id, Message ID 
 u_char id_volume, Volume (0~127)  
 u_int time) Transition time (0~65535) (msec)  
Calling conditions 
Can be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Not multithread safe (must be called in interrupt-disabled state)  
Note that this function can be called in a multithreaded environment in an interrupt-enabled state if it does 
not conflict with other sceSESq2 function calls. 
Description 
This function sets the volume of a sound effect sequence that is being performed, which is specified by the 
message ID.  
The volume is changed from its value at the time the performance starts to the volume specified by the 
argument, during the indicated transition time. 
If this function is called for a performance whose volume is already changing, the starting volume used for 
the new transition will be the volume at the time the function is called. 
Return value 
Table 13-24 
Value Result 
SCE_OK Normal termination 
 
If an error occurs, the low-order 16 bits of the return value indicate one of the following values. 
Table 13-25 
Value Result 
EINVAL Invalid argument 
EFAULT Invalid Module Context address 
EFORMAT Invalid Module Context  
SCE_EREGIST Address of the ID monitor structure has not been registered 
SCE_ENOT_PLAYED Specified message_id is not being performed

13-24       CSL Sound Effect Sequencer 2 - Control Functions 
 
PlayStation 2 IOP Library Reference Release 3.0.2  -  Sound Libraries 
sceSESq2SetPitch 
Set pitch of sound effect sequence 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
modsesq2 3.0.1 March 31, 2004 
Syntax 
int sceSESq2SetPitch(  
 sceCslCtx *module_context, Address of Module Context 
 u_int message_id, Message ID 
 short id_pitch, Pitch (-1200~1200)  
 u_int time) Transition time (0 ~65535) (msec)  
Calling conditions 
Can be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Not multithread safe (must be called in interrupt-disabled state)  
Note that this function can be called in a multithreaded environment in an interrupt-enabled state if it does 
not conflict with other sceSESq2 function calls. 
Description 
This function sets the pitch of a sound effect sequence that is being performed, which is specified by the 
message ID.  
Pitch is specified in units of cents, and there are 100 cents in one halftone. Pitch change is specified as an 
absolute value relative to the original pitch. 
The pitch is changed from its value when the performance starts to the specified pitch during the indicated 
transition time. Note that the final pitch must be within the range permitted by the SPU2. 
If this function is called for a performance whose pitch is already changing, the starting pitch used for the 
new transition will be the pitch at the time the function is called. 
Return value 
Table 13-28 
Value Result 
SCE_OK Normal termination 
 
If an error occurs, the low-order 16 bits of the return value indicate one of the following values. 
Table 13-29 
Value Result 
EINVAL Invalid argument 
EFAULT Invalid Module Context address 
EFORMAT Invalid Module Context  
SCE_EREGISTER Address of the ID monitor structure has not been registered   
SCE_ENOT_PLAYED Specified message_id is not being performed
